DOCUMENT docs/assessment-production/standalone-debates-v1/debate-308/publication/contract.md
# Isolated publication writer

Read your complete execution plan and its entire lossless reader, including every shared block, in bounded untruncated outputs. Only the plan and reader are allowed evidence. Do not inspect peers, sessions, credentials, other debates or environment; do not browse, make paid calls or spawn agents. Report complete reading and the sole absolute output, then wait for controller authenticated reading release before authoring. Use the frozen source, resolved judgments, calculated scores and skeleton. Preserve identity, motion, scope, section/card order, times, numbers and assessment substance. Reference profiles provide a comparison corpus, never debate evidence or reusable prose.

Output exactly {summary,quotes,moves,overall,logicalExtension,noveltyMap,editorialReview}. Do not output another assembled candidate. The checker inserts these leaves into the immutable skeleton.

- summary: 8–35 words describing the assessed exchange and its result fairly.
- quotes: {pro:{text,context},con:{text,context}}. Text is a verbatim frozen quoteEligibleExactSpans string of that side, 3–18 words; preserve source case and punctuation. Read each without its context: it must express a complete thought, retaining any meaning-changing qualification. Contexts are 12–55 words; they explain use, not complete a fragment. Prefer clearly articulated claims over pronoun-dependent snippets or biographical remarks.
- moves: exactly 19 entries in skeleton publication order (section, exchange, pro then con), each {moveId,words,critique}. Description normally 22–28 words: actual claim, support/comparison and implication, without padding or invented premises. Mean at least20.9; no more than25% below20words. No tags yet.
- Each critique has exactly four sentences, beginning Strongest feature:, Principal limitation:, Live burden:, Locked score:, in that order. 105–130 words AND at least880 characters; target112–122 substantive words while satisfying both gates. The last sentence states the exact card score as N/100 with a specific explanation. Every sentence ends with terminal punctuation; avoid internal full stops in abbreviations. Discuss the actual inference, evidence, limitation and remaining burden. Do not turn withholding assent into an obligation to prove the contrary. Write source-specific prose manually, without interchangeable sentence-bank bodies, padded suffixes or repeated templates. Common six-word sequences present in at least one-quarter of critiques may cover at most15% of words on average and25% in any critique.
- overall: {pro:{strengths:[...],blunders:[{text,links:[]},...]},con:...}. At least3 concrete strengths and2 material blunders each, normally18–35words per item. Remain score-consistent; no invented claims, new numerical penalties or speculative labels. Links remain empty until separate rhetorical review.
- logicalExtension: {pro:{finalArgument:{thesis,premises,conclusion},newArguments:[{title,text},...]},con:...}. Thesis at least12words; four to six premises each at least12words; conclusion at least15words. Two to four distinct reinforcing arguments per side, title2–8words, text45–130words, preferably80–110 substantive words. The whole section is visibly labeled AI Contribution. Within it write direct philosophical prose, without repeated provenance or generic model/constraint framing. Strengthen both sides proportionately, distinguish new reasoning from recorded contributions, and preserve the question and opponent burden. Each reinforcing argument needs a genuinely new inference beyond the inventory. Preserve natural punctuation. Compare both sides' openings, sentence structures and conclusions for mirrored scaffolds and noun-swappable prose; compare the supplied independent corpus without recycling it.
- noveltyMap: one {side,title,closestMoveIds:[...],distinctInferentialContribution} for every reinforcing argument. Identify a concrete inferential addition, not a generic novelty assertion.
- editorialReview: {sourceFidelity,completeQuotes,scoreConsistency,nonformulaicCritiques,aiContributionNonformulaic,aiPunctuation,referenceComparison}. Each is a source-specific string at least40characters describing a review actually performed.

## Frozen source constraints

# Frozen source and scope

Exact question: Is religious disagreement evidence against God’s existence? Pro is Justin Schieber; con is Randal Rauser. Justin Brierley is the host. The programme’s January 14, 2017 listing establishes identity and date only. Outside scholarship and assessments are unavailable as judging evidence.

The controller read all 1,596 canonical caption events. Canonical event indices are ZERO-BASED, distinct from the maintained reader’s ONE-BASED line labels. The automatic transcript is unchanged. Complete coverage includes excluded introductory and promotional material for boundary auditing only.

Assess events 319–613, 679–1295 and 1338–1567, restricted to primary-speaker argument words. The audible scope is approximately 12:41–1:00:59; caption overlap and mixed events extend the technical window slightly around these handoffs. Event 319 starts with the host’s “of make your case there”, followed by Schieber’s “yes so in”. Event 679 begins with the host’s “to that kind of stuff”, then Rauser says “yeah I like how”. Event 1295 begins with Rauser’s final “evidence”, followed by host break language. Event 1338 begins with host “towards the end of today’s show”, then Schieber’s “sure so”. Event 1567 ends Rauser’s “disagreement that we presently find”, then the host’s “it’s”. None of these host fragments may be scored or quoted as a guest’s words.

Use the explicit editor-approved moderator-context lane. Brierley’s questions, premises, examples, endorsements and summaries are unscored. A guest’s actual answer remains eligible. Do not silently attribute a host argument to a guest or manufacture an opponent reply target for a host prompt. A later guest explicitly adopting a premise may be judged on that guest’s own stated reasoning. Consider unequal prompting and the absence of a further reply opportunity; no penalty merely because a guest did not answer the other’s final reply.

Brierley introduces prevalence of God-belief as possible theistic evidence at 32:49–33:35, presses independent doctrinal convergence using a fine-tuning/design analogy at 49:44–50:10, frames the argument against other evidence at 55:33–56:33, invokes Jesus’s prayer for unity at 58:19–58:52, and offers a UK ecumenical improvement example at 1:00:03–1:00:31. Those are context only. His earlier team-building, salvation and later ad-hoc summaries track arguments already offered by guests; they do not add scored moves. The contract requires a move-by-move moderatorContextAudit.

Schieber’s landlord analogy distinguishes fundamental conflicting religious commitments from trivial disagreements whose resolution can develop virtues. He expressly denies that his argument assumes God personally delivers contradictory revelations: people claim revelation, and the issue is why a God concerned with truth and relationship would not correct consequential errors. Preserve this clarification at 21:59–22:34 and 33:35–34:19 when evaluating Rauser’s response.

At 19:38–22:34 the speaker is Schieber, despite the preceding host question addressing Rauser. First-person reference to the argument, the toddlers example and subsequent host handoff back to Randal establish this attribution. The host at 22:35 attributes the example to Justin. Distinguish Justin Schieber from Justin Brierley everywhere; shared first names alone do not establish ownership. If remaining selected attribution is materially uncertain, mark it below high and require audio verification before adjudication.

Rauser replies with possible sufficient reasons, a team-building exercise, developmental teaching, general moral knowledge and inclusivist judgment according to available evidence. He does not claim all conflicting doctrines are true or that doctrinal knowledge never matters. His grammar example is an analogy for staged learning; caption corruption of the spelling mnemonic is not an argumentative defect.

Schieber frames the case as comparative evidence or best explanation, expressly not logical contradiction, at 29:40–30:16 and 41:53–42:35. Near 56:33–57:32 he explicitly permits other theistic arguments to outweigh it and denies that it makes belief in God irrational by deduction. Do not assign him proof that every possible divine reason is impossible. Rauser need not independently prove God exists; distinguish logical compatibility from comparative explanatory force when evaluating his replies.

Rauser’s exclusivism objection says theism need not require correct doctrines for salvation. Schieber rejects exclusivism as a premise and stresses false beliefs, impaired relationships and tensions among believers, including conflict and parental restrictions. Do not erase the dispute by silently adopting either reconstruction or treat his caliphate example as a claim about all Muslims.

On relationship, Rauser distinguishes propositional knowledge from acquaintance and developmental relationship, using infant–mother and coworker contrasts. Schieber grants some substantive relationship without mutual awareness but ranks freely chosen mutual awareness higher and asks why God would settle for less. Preserve both concessions; do not attribute denial of all infant–parent value or a settled hierarchy that neither defended.

For hypothetical independent doctrinal convergence, Schieber distinguishes broad supernatural belief from detailed doctrines such as Trinity, divine simplicity and atonement. Rauser proposes possible natural convergence and later invokes projection theories and broad cross-cultural similarities. His final separate point is that greater agreement supporting God would not by itself show current disagreement supports atheism. Account for this closing inference independently; do not collapse specificity, generality, logical negation and comparative evidence.

Rauser’s cognitive-wiring/autism claim at 48:33–49:03 and his evolutionary examples are speaker claims, not independently verified facts or a basis for stereotyping. Likewise Schieber’s evolutionary-literature claim and Rauser’s references to Feuerbach, Freud and Marx receive no outside substantiation. Judge only the warrant actually supplied. Named authorities are not automatic evidence of correctness.

Both concluding replies matter: Schieber defends detailed doctrinal specificity against natural convergence at 52:30–53:58 and qualifies cumulative force at 56:33–57:32; Rauser distinguishes higher agreement from current evidence at 55:09–55:28, then discusses continuing Christian differences and charitable dialogue at 58:52–1:00:59. Repetition may be covered without adding a new move, but a new load-bearing distinction must not be lost.

Automatic captions commonly misrecognize names and terms: Rauser, Schieber, theism, abductive, acquaintance, inclusivism, Feuerbach and irenic. Never penalize recognition errors or silently repair exact quotations. Caption events overlap in time; source spans are contiguous text windows whose mixed-speaker fragments need explicit ownership restrictions. No audio check or direct listening has yet occurred. Any selected uncertainty material to attribution or meaning requires below-high confidence and subsequent verification.

Featured quotations must be exact 3–18 word complete thoughts with clear referents and essential qualifications. Do not crop a concession away from its challenge, quote a dangling clause, silently fix caption wording or invent completion words. Select a different complete excerpt if the original will not fit. At least one suitable candidate per side must be available before inventory lock.

DOCUMENT docs/assessment-production/standalone-debates-v1/debate-308/source/assessment-source-notes.md
# Frozen source and scope

Exact question: Is religious disagreement evidence against God’s existence? Pro is Justin Schieber; con is Randal Rauser. Justin Brierley is the host. The programme’s January 14, 2017 listing establishes identity and date only. Outside scholarship and assessments are unavailable as judging evidence.

The controller read all 1,596 canonical caption events. Canonical event indices are ZERO-BASED, distinct from the maintained reader’s ONE-BASED line labels. The automatic transcript is unchanged. Complete coverage includes excluded introductory and promotional material for boundary auditing only.

Assess events 319–613, 679–1295 and 1338–1567, restricted to primary-speaker argument words. The audible scope is approximately 12:41–1:00:59; caption overlap and mixed events extend the technical window slightly around these handoffs. Event 319 starts with the host’s “of make your case there”, followed by Schieber’s “yes so in”. Event 679 begins with the host’s “to that kind of stuff”, then Rauser says “yeah I like how”. Event 1295 begins with Rauser’s final “evidence”, followed by host break language. Event 1338 begins with host “towards the end of today’s show”, then Schieber’s “sure so”. Event 1567 ends Rauser’s “disagreement that we presently find”, then the host’s “it’s”. None of these host fragments may be scored or quoted as a guest’s words.

Use the explicit editor-approved moderator-context lane. Brierley’s questions, premises, examples, endorsements and summaries are unscored. A guest’s actual answer remains eligible. Do not silently attribute a host argument to a guest or manufacture an opponent reply target for a host prompt. A later guest explicitly adopting a premise may be judged on that guest’s own stated reasoning. Consider unequal prompting and the absence of a further reply opportunity; no penalty merely because a guest did not answer the other’s final reply.

Brierley introduces prevalence of God-belief as possible theistic evidence at 32:49–33:35, presses independent doctrinal convergence using a fine-tuning/design analogy at 49:44–50:10, frames the argument against other evidence at 55:33–56:33, invokes Jesus’s prayer for unity at 58:19–58:52, and offers a UK ecumenical improvement example at 1:00:03–1:00:31. Those are context only. His earlier team-building, salvation and later ad-hoc summaries track arguments already offered by guests; they do not add scored moves. The contract requires a move-by-move moderatorContextAudit.

Schieber’s landlord analogy distinguishes fundamental conflicting religious commitments from trivial disagreements whose resolution can develop virtues. He expressly denies that his argument assumes God personally delivers contradictory revelations: people claim revelation, and the issue is why a God concerned with truth and relationship would not correct consequential errors. Preserve this clarification at 21:59–22:34 and 33:35–34:19 when evaluating Rauser’s response.

At 19:38–22:34 the speaker is Schieber, despite the preceding host question addressing Rauser. First-person reference to the argument, the toddlers example and subsequent host handoff back to Randal establish this attribution. The host at 22:35 attributes the example to Justin. Distinguish Justin Schieber from Justin Brierley everywhere; shared first names alone do not establish ownership. If remaining selected attribution is materially uncertain, mark it below high and require audio verification before adjudication.

Rauser replies with possible sufficient reasons, a team-building exercise, developmental teaching, general moral knowledge and inclusivist judgment according to available evidence. He does not claim all conflicting doctrines are true or that doctrinal knowledge never matters. His grammar example is an analogy for staged learning; caption corruption of the spelling mnemonic is not an argumentative defect.

Schieber frames the case as comparative evidence or best explanation, expressly not logical contradiction, at 29:40–30:16 and 41:53–42:35. Near 56:33–57:32 he explicitly permits other theistic arguments to outweigh it and denies that it makes belief in God irrational by deduction. Do not assign him proof that every possible divine reason is impossible. Rauser need not independently prove God exists; distinguish logical compatibility from comparative explanatory force when evaluating his replies.

Rauser’s exclusivism objection says theism need not require correct doctrines for salvation. Schieber rejects exclusivism as a premise and stresses false beliefs, impaired relationships and tensions among believers, including conflict and parental restrictions. Do not erase the dispute by silently adopting either reconstruction or treat his caliphate example as a claim about all Muslims.

On relationship, Rauser distinguishes propositional knowledge from acquaintance and developmental relationship, using infant–mother and coworker contrasts. Schieber grants some substantive relationship without mutual awareness but ranks freely chosen mutual awareness higher and asks why God would settle for less. Preserve both concessions; do not attribute denial of all infant–parent value or a settled hierarchy that neither defended.

For hypothetical independent doctrinal convergence, Schieber distinguishes broad supernatural belief from detailed doctrines such as Trinity, divine simplicity and atonement. Rauser proposes possible natural convergence and later invokes projection theories and broad cross-cultural similarities. His final separate point is that greater agreement supporting God would not by itself show current disagreement supports atheism. Account for this closing inference independently; do not collapse specificity, generality, logical negation and comparative evidence.

Rauser’s cognitive-wiring/autism claim at 48:33–49:03 and his evolutionary examples are speaker claims, not independently verified facts or a basis for stereotyping. Likewise Schieber’s evolutionary-literature claim and Rauser’s references to Feuerbach, Freud and Marx receive no outside substantiation. Judge only the warrant actually supplied. Named authorities are not automatic evidence of correctness.

Both concluding replies matter: Schieber defends detailed doctrinal specificity against natural convergence at 52:30–53:58 and qualifies cumulative force at 56:33–57:32; Rauser distinguishes higher agreement from current evidence at 55:09–55:28, then discusses continuing Christian differences and charitable dialogue at 58:52–1:00:59. Repetition may be covered without adding a new move, but a new load-bearing distinction must not be lost.

Automatic captions commonly misrecognize names and terms: Rauser, Schieber, theism, abductive, acquaintance, inclusivism, Feuerbach and irenic. Never penalize recognition errors or silently repair exact quotations. Caption events overlap in time; source spans are contiguous text windows whose mixed-speaker fragments need explicit ownership restrictions. No audio check or direct listening has yet occurred. Any selected uncertainty material to attribution or meaning requires below-high confidence and subsequent verification.

Featured quotations must be exact 3–18 word complete thoughts with clear referents and essential qualifications. Do not crop a concession away from its challenge, quote a dangling clause, silently fix caption wording or invent completion words. Select a different complete excerpt if the original will not fit. At least one suitable candidate per side must be available before inventory lock.

END DOCUMENT
